- [-] MSBuild beyond the files the doc engine decodes, and its solutions: measured against dotnet msbuild (MSBuild 18.9.11 in .NET SDK 10.0.401; -getItem, -getProperty and -preprocess; tier A, definitions, references and cross-file references) on App-vNext/Polly 8.8.0 (34 files, the whole repository) and dotnet/aspnetcore v10.0.12 (src/Http: 41 files, 40 projects and the props and targets they import), 2026-09-29: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (75 files; the 4 MSBuildTarget entities are counted under excluded_by_kind), ref_recall 1 and ref_precision .9688 (62 pairs; the 2 dialect-only references are System.Memory under a Windows-only target framework that a macOS run does not evaluate, 3 such declarations in all: the harness compares only files that hold a reference in the tool's truth, so a third file with none is left out), xref_precision 1 and xref_recall .9474 (36 of 38 edges; both misses are written from $(RepoRoot)), so L1, L2 and L3 excellent {id: LB-Q-msbuild-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "the queue entry names .sln and this dialect reads none: a solution is a line-based text format and not one of the doc engine's decoders (YAML, JSON, TOML, XML), and .slnx is XML whose root is a <Solution> the engine cannot tell from a <Project> (a decoded root carries no element name), so no solution and no solution-to-project edge is indexed and `dotnet sln list` is not an oracle here; property expansion is not modelled, so a ProjectReference written with $(RepoRoot) or $(MSBuildThisFileDirectory) resolves to nothing (2 of 38 oracle edges) and no <Import> edge is drawn at all (Polly and aspnetcore write theirs with a property), a declaration under a false condition is still a reference (the oracle unions the Debug and Release evaluations of every framework the tool lists, so a framework a macOS run cannot evaluate stays out of its truth), a Directory.Build.props is imported implicitly and its items belong to the importing project for the tool and to the props file here, aspnetcore's <Reference Include=assembly> items that eng/targets/ResolveReferences.targets turns into ProjectReferences (12 oracle edges, attributed to that targets file, outside src/Http) are not modelled, and the central package versions (PackageVersion, GlobalPackageReference), Choose/When blocks, items inside a target, properties and a target's own DependsOnTargets, BeforeTargets and AfterTargets are not entities or references; aspnetcore cannot be evaluated from a clean checkout (its root Directory.Build.props and .targets import artifacts/bin/GenerateFiles/*, which its own first build step writes), so the oracle run stubs both as empty projects", trigger: "a solution decoder in the doc engine (or a root-element selector for .slnx) and a property-expanding value for ProjectReference and Import; then .sln joins the oracle through dotnet sln list, Import edges join the truth and the reading is re-measured"}

### Wave 3 — the next tier, components and the build/CI family

- [x] Kustomize (kustomization.yaml) — tool format at L3 (doc engine; pure-go:gopkg.in/yaml.v3, n/a; oracle: kustomize build (tier A); retires kustomizeextract.go) {id: LB-Q-kustomize, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/kustomize.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[34]\"", needs: "LB-F8,LB-F9,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F21,LB-F22,LB-F18,LB-Q-kubernetes"}
- [-] Kustomize beyond the base and overlay directories an ingest resolves today: measured against Kustomize v5.8.1 (kubectl v1.36.1's embedded build; tier A, definitions, references and cross-file references, each entry kept only when the build changes without it) on kubernetes-sigs/kustomize kustomize/v5.8.1 (examples: 18 kustomizations, 16 built by the tool) and argoproj/argocd-example-apps 8088f4c (4 files, 3 built), 2026-09-29: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (89 definitions), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (70 entries, every one evidenced by the tool), xref_precision and xref_recall 1 (13 of 13 edges), so L1, L2 and L3 excellent; a scratch ingest of both subjects stores the same 13 edges {id: LB-Q-kustomize-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "a directory a resource or base names resolves to the one doc file in it (design section 4), and that holds today only because an ingest dispatches a Kubernetes manifest to the incumbent K8sExtractor, which is not a doc-engine extractor: the day the kubernetes dialect is dispatched every base that holds a manifest holds two doc files and its overlay edge stops resolving (TestKustomizeBaseDirectoryNeedsTheOneFileRule pins the rule); a resource that names a manifest is recorded but resolves to nothing because no manifest has a root entity in the store yet, and the harness scores only edges between the files the dialect owns (53 oracle edges to manifests are out of scope); a remote base or patch names no path of the repository (loadHttp is left out of the measure, and no build offline can follow it); the tool cannot build 3 of the 22 kustomizations (a remote base, kustomizeconfig/mykind.yaml and all.yaml are not in the checkouts) so they are not measured; transformers, generators, validators, configurations, crds, replacements, replicas and helmCharts are not entities or references; a patch is signed by its target name and an image by its newName because the doc engine has no concatenation; the measure is small (19 files, 13 base or overlay edges) and both subjects are documentation and demonstration trees", trigger: "fusion.ResolveDocXrefs preferring the file the referring dialect owns when a directory holds several doc files (needed before the kubernetes dialect is dispatched) and a manifest root entity that a resource entry can resolve to; then the reading is re-measured on a larger subject with components, transformers and overlays of overlays"}
- [ ] Assembly (.s .S .asm) — language at L1 (tags engine; RubixDev/tree-sitter-asm, MIT; oracle: ctags) {id: LB-Q-assembly, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/assembly.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[1234]\"", needs: "LB-F1,LB-F2,LB-F3,LB-F4,LB-F12,LB-F13,LB-F15,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F20,LB-F21,LB-F22"}
- [-] Assembly refused (design §14.2): no dialect was built and no level is claimed; .s, .S and .asm stay disclosed as unsupported. Measured 2026-09-29 by parsing every calibration file with the grammar and counting those whose tree holds an ERROR or MISSING node (calibration/corpus.d/assembly.yaml: cfenollosa/os-tutorial master 8002382, 71 NASM .asm files; BLAKE3-team/BLAKE3 1.8.7 c/, 8 GAS .S and 4 MASM .asm files; universal-ctags 6.2.1 reads all three syntaxes with one parser, so the L1 oracle was never the obstacle) {id: LB-Q-assembly-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real assembly under the 5% line: 69 of the 83 calibration files (83.1%: os-tutorial 57 of 71, BLAKE3 12 of 12) parse with an error under RubixDev/tree-sitter-asm v0.24.0 (5bb5b03) and under upstream main 839741f (2025-11-08, six commits later and the best pin measured, which changed neither count); it is one generic grammar and lacks the common shapes of all three dialects: GAS directives with arguments (.p2align 6, .section .note.GNU-stack,'',%progbits, .type f, @function, .equ N, 8), NASM [org 0x7c00], %include, $ and $$, quoted data (db 'hi', 0), MASM quoted segment classes ('CODE'). Re-plan when a tree-sitter grammar (or one per dialect: GAS, NASM, MASM) parses the calibration subjects with at most 5% of files in error: pin it, then build the dialect (global labels and macros, local labels .L* and numeric 1: excluded, universal-ctags Asm kinds define, label, macro, type through oracle_kinds), dispatch .s .S .asm (an ExtractorEpoch bump) and measure L1 at tier B"}

- [-] Argo CD Applications and Workflows beyond L3 good, now the Argo CD kinds and the engine gaps: wave ox (2026-10-02) measured the Workflow kinds at tier A on argoproj/argo-workflows v4.1.4 examples (212 files: argo lint --offline 4.1.4 with the subject's WorkflowTemplates as context, argo-workflows' own v1alpha1 types strict, lint ablation for every reference and templateRef): sym_recall 1, sym_precision .9986 (1,428 of 1,430; 1 file the strict decode refuses), ref and xref precision and recall 1 (583 references, 23 cross-file edges, all attested), level_met L3 excellent; the Argo CD kinds stay unmeasured at tier A {id: LB-Q-argo-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "an Application, ApplicationSet or AppProject has no offline tool, so the Argo CD kinds stay out of the tier-A reading (their tier-C reading of 2026-10-02 agreed on all 64 definitions, with no cross-file edge that has a measured truth); a dag task's `depends:` expression and `{{tasks.x.outputs}}` need string-interpolation capture, which the engine lacks (appfw's optional pattern capture); a templateRef to a WorkflowTemplate that writes metadata.namespace is named WorkflowTemplate/<ns>/<name> and no selector reaches it from a nested step; an Application's source.path resolves only to a directory of this repository holding exactly one Argo file and names a directory of another repository (repoURL) in every Application of the subjects, so the path and chart edges have no measured truth; argocd-example-apps ships no LICENSE file (the argoproj organisation's Apache-2.0 is the declaration, as the kustomize shard records it)", trigger: "an offline tool that evaluates Argo CD Applications, ApplicationSets and AppProjects (then argo-cd and argocd-example-apps rejoin the tier-A reading); appfw's pattern capture lands (depends and {{tasks.x}} as L2 references); a node-parent namespace in the doc engine (a nested reference reading the object's namespace); a subject whose Applications name directories of their own repository, with one manifest file each"}

- [ ] Groovy (.groovy .gvy) — language at L2 (tags engine; murtaza64/tree-sitter-groovy, verify at pin; oracle: tree-sitter-tags (C)) {id: LB-Q-groovy, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/groovy.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[234]\"", needs: "LB-F1,LB-F2,LB-F3,LB-F4,LB-F12,LB-F13,LB-F15,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F20,LB-F21,LB-F22"}
- [-] Groovy refused (design §14.2): no dialect is built and no level is claimed; .groovy and .gvy stay disclosed as unsupported. Measured 2026-10-01 with the tree-sitter 0.27 CLI over every .groovy and .gvy file of the queue's subjects (calibration/corpus.d/groovy.yaml: spockframework/spock spock-2.4, 576 files; apache/grails-core v7.2.4, 5,132), a file counting when its tree holds an ERROR or MISSING node; universal-ctags 6.2.1 has no Groovy parser, so L1 would have been tier C {id: LB-Q-groovy-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real Groovy under the 5% line: murtaza64/tree-sitter-groovy main 2a6ddd55 (the queue's grammar, MIT, the most recent commit 2026-09-27) leaves an error in 3,642 of 5,708 files (63.8%: spock 331 of 576, grails-core 3,311 of 5,132), dekobon/tree-sitter-groovy v0.3.0 (654e4c2d, Apache-2.0 or MIT, the best of the four on Groovy sources) in 2,466 (43.2%), Decodetalkers/tree-sitter-groovy master 679459be (MIT, generated with the CLI) in 4,326 (75.8%) and amaanq/tree-sitter-groovy v0.1.2 (70efb0b9, MIT declared) in 4,875 (85.4%); the language-pack catalogue's Decodetalkers pin a6cf8f80 carries no licence at that commit and lang-pin -tree refuses it; the grammars read Groovy as a scripting language and fail on its class syntax: an implements clause, a constructor, a throws clause, annotation arguments (named values, class literals), wildcard generics (Closure<?>). Re-plan when a tree-sitter Groovy grammar parses both subjects with at most 5% of files in error: pin it, build the dialect (classes, interfaces, traits, enums, methods, constructors, fields, closures assigned to names), dispatch .groovy and .gvy (an ExtractorEpoch bump) and measure L1 and L2 at tier C"}

Measured 2026-10-09 on laravel/laravel v13.11.0, symfony/demo v3.1.0 and laravel/framework v13.35.0 (its 40 composer.json, a sparse checkout): sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (51 definitions: 40 packages and 11 scripts), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (424 pairs: requires, require-dev and `@script` references), so L1 and L2 excellent. ingest reads composer.json by basename through the dialect (LB-Q-composer) {id: LB-Q-composer, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/composer.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[234]\"", needs: "LB-F8,LB-F9,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F21,LB-F22"}
- [-] Composer beyond L2: the autoload references (psr-4, psr-0, classmap and files directories as repo-path references, in both autoload and autoload-dev) are extracted and unmeasured, and a script reference to `@php`, `@composer` or `@putenv` is told from a script reference by the document's declared scripts alone {id: LB-Q-composer-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L3 is not attempted: the cross-reference family resolves a repo-path only to the one doc file in the named directory (the nested composer.json of a path repository), and the oracle for the rest is Composer's own class-to-file resolution (the authoritative classmap of `composer dump-autoload`), which names a PHP file, not a doc entity; the subjects keep their autoload directories (src/, app/) as PHP, which the php dialect reads; a script named php, composer or putenv would be mistaken for the target of `@php ...`, which Composer runs as a command (the pattern has no lookahead); a dependency is a reference by name, so the platform packages php and ext-* count as references as Composer lists them", trigger: "the PHP namespace-directory join (a psr-4 prefix to the PHP files under its directory, feeding the php dialect) is built, so a class-to-file reading at tier A (dump-autoload's classmap) can grade the autoload references"}

For the record, on an uncommitted scratch manifest that compares classes and functions only (no reading, no level): sym_recall .8758, sym_precision .9907, kind_agreement 1, signature_coverage 1, 2.45 MB/CPU-s, 433 peak module pages, 0 faults (dialect notes: internal/extractor/dialects/matlab/PROVENANCE.md); ctags also misses 12 classes and writes no MatLab end line, so range_agreement would cap L1 at good even with an oracle for the kinds", trigger: "an L1 oracle that reports the methods and properties of a classdef (a MATLAB parser dump or a SCIP producer): measure the four required kinds against it, then flip LB-Q-matlab; L2 needs a call oracle as well"}

- [-] Scala at L2 (L1 good, L2 good), below its L4 target, and `.scala` and `.sc` served by the tags dialect: measured 2026-10-10 (wave lf3, fathom bc499d5d) against a scalameta 4.17.4 syntax dump (tier B, definitions and calls, calibration/oracles/scalameta) on typelevel/cats v2.13.0 + http4s/http4s v0.23.38, 1,502 files: sym_recall 1, sym_precision 1, kind_agreement 1, signature_coverage 1, range_agreement .8042, call_recall .9999, call_precision .9999; lang-parity MERGE against scalaextract.go, which is retired (F.54); L3 first read 2026-10-10 (wave lf4 item fileedge, fathom 35bff928, once, K19) against the dump's imports and same-package references (LB-F439): import_resolution .9832, file_edge_precision .9763, file_edge_recall .0112 {id: LB-Q-scala-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L1 is good on range_agreement alone (.8042, 21,084 of 26,218; bar .90): tree-sitter-scala ends an indentation-delimited node (a body on the line after =, a Scala 3 template) at the outdent before the next token, and the tags engine counts a node to its last byte, so such a definition ends on the next definition's line; trimming trailing whitespace in tsEndLine, the engine's file (item tsjs), reads .9511 (O761). L2 is capped at good by its tier-B oracle: no tier-A call oracle runs here. L3 is unmet on file_edge_recall .0112 (329 of 29,261; bar .25) with import_resolution excellent (.9832, LB-F438) and precision .9763: the dialect has no references.scm and stores a call by its callee's last identifier, so a resolved import becomes a file edge only where its name is called bare; 22,563 of the 28,932 undrawn pairs are a wildcard's pairs to every file of a package, 4,292 same-package references, 2,077 named imports (O807). L4 needs scip-java's SemanticDB index, which needs each subject compiled by its own sbt build: sbt is not installed here, and a coursier-launched sbt compiling cross-built cats or http4s for scip-java's SemanticDB is over the wave's one-JVM, 1 GB heap budget (sbt's own launcher defaults to 1 GB before scalac runs), so it was not attempted. universal-ctags 6.2.1 has no Scala parser, so the queue's ctags oracle never existed (O763)", trigger: "the engine's end line trimmed (O761), which reads L1 excellent; a Scala references.scm and qualified call text (`calls.qualify`) under K17/K26's cost gate, then L3 re-read on a holdout; an sbt or Mill build of a Scala subject indexed by scip-java (SemanticDB) for L4"}

- [-] Objective-C at L3 good (L1 good, L2 good, L3 good), below L4, and `.mm`, `.m` and the Objective-C `.h` served by the tags dialect: measured 2026-10-20 (wave lang6, fathom 486ff36e) against universal-ctags 6.2.1 and clang-objc (libclang through the Command Line Tools, tier B) on AFNetworking 4.0.1 + SDWebImage 5.21.7, 203 files the compiler parses clean on this host: sym_recall .9337, sym_precision .952, kind_agreement .9942, signature_coverage 1, range_agreement .9996, call_recall .9947, call_precision .9754, import_resolution 1, file_edge_precision .9974, file_edge_recall .5951; held out (K19, once, calibration/corpus.d/objective-c-holdout.yaml, pinned before any reading): ReactiveObjC 3.1.1, 161 files, L3 good (sym_recall .916, sym_precision .9227, call_recall .9897, call_precision .9739, import_resolution 1, file_edge_precision 1, file_edge_recall .45) {id: LB-Q-objective-c-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L1 is good, not excellent, on sym_recall .9337 and the holdout's .916 (bar .95): the misses are universal-ctags' own more than the dialect's (it tags an enumerator as a typedef, a class extension's first word as a category, a method under an availability macro as the macro's name) and the C prototypes a header declares, which the dialect, like C's, does not emit. L2 and L3 are capped at good by their tier-B oracle: the subjects ship no compile database (AFNetworking builds with CocoaPods and Carthage, SDWebImage with Xcode and SwiftPM), so clang-objc's include roots are the run's and a file's conditional code is read under two configurations, macOS and Mac Catalyst, not the project's build. L4 needs an Objective-C-capable SCIP or index-store producer: scip-clang 0.4.0 indexes C and C++ declarations only and its driver drops .m and .mm database entries (O1413), so no def_mapping or typed_recall can be measured. 78 of the subjects' 341 translation units do not parse on this host (the Command Line Tools SDK has no XCTest or WatchKit; a module map names a missing file) and are out of every level's scope, named in the oracle's errored list; the 47 of 203 measured files that hold an error as written are read through the objc-macros step (NS_ENUM and its siblings, availability and export macros), which leaves 3 (1.5%, under the 5% line). A selector ends in a colon, which store.CalleeName cuts at (O1414)", trigger: "an Objective-C SCIP indexer (an index-store reader, or a scip-clang with an Objective-C visitor) run on a subject with its Xcode or SwiftPM build, for L4; a compile_commands.json from a real build (xcodebuild with a compilation database, or a bear capture) for tier-A L2 and L3; a store CalleeName that keeps a selector whole (O1414)"}

Also unread: a .svelte import of a .ts module draws no file edge in ingest (the file resolver's universe is the host's own files, O979). K29 at wave lf7's integration (d3f748e8): tsx's owner ruling S3a makes TypeScript's module-level let a JSConst too, so shadcn-svelte's TypeScript scripts read 3,170 definitions (were 1,229) and kit's 475 (were 428), and svelte.json re-read reads sym_precision .3816, level_met none, L2 and L3 excellent unchanged", trigger: "the JavaScript and TypeScript dialects read a let or var as a canonical variable (a row with its own K29 re-read), or an owner ruling on K19's let rule for components (since owner rulings M1 and S3a both dialects name a module-level let a JSConst); then svelte.json is re-read once on its subjects"}

The cross-file truth is tier C, which caps at good (cfn-lint resolves no nested template, no identity join and no handler; an independent tier-A or tier-B resolver of a SAM application's handlers, sam build's own, needs each runtime's toolchain). And 25 of the 404 handler bindings are missed, every one a node handler the javascript dialect does not extract (an export whose value is a wrapper call, module.exports.x = async () => {}: O721, the javascript dialect's to fix), which also caps xref_recall under the .95 a tier-A truth would ask of excellent. Not read: a Fn::Sub's ${} names outside the CloudWatch properties, Fn::FindInMap, Fn::ImportValue and Export (cross-stack), the go, provided, dotnet and custom runtimes' handlers (a binary or an assembly), a template whose YAML cfn-lint rejects (O722)", trigger: "a tier-A or tier-B handler or nested-template resolver an oracle can run offline, or O721 fixed in the javascript dialect"}

126 units Terragrunt could not evaluate and 13 files the parser rejects are left out), sym_recall and sym_precision 1, range_agreement 1 (3460 entities), ref_recall 1 and ref_precision 1 (362 pairs), xref_precision 0.9876 and xref_recall 0.9917 (479 of 483 truth pairs found, 479 of 485 found in truth) against python-hcl2 8.1.4 (tier C: definitions and references) and terragrunt 1.1.6 render --json --with-metadata and find --json --include (tier A: include, dependency, terraform.source and input edges) {id: LB-Q-terragrunt-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L1 and L2 capped at good: no tool reports a Terragrunt file's entities or the references between them with lines, so the independent parse is tier C (§1.3); wave ox measured a tier-A attestation (calibration/oracles/terragrunt/terragrunt_attest.py: render --with-metadata found_in_file for blocks, inputs and a unit's own locals, find --include for includes, ablation for an included file's locals, feature blocks, unnamed files and every reference; names and lines from python-hcl2, since fathom's dialect and Terragrunt share hashicorp/hcl/v2): it evidences all 74 definitions and 20 references of the live example but only 3,056 of 3,571 definitions and 246 of 342 references of test/fixtures (152 terragrunt.stack.hcl files render does not read, 78 other files no rendered unit names, 10 units that do not render, 174 included-file locals and 96 references no rendered output depends on), so the families stay at C, locals with them; L3 misses 4 truth pairs: a config_path read from another include (include.x.dependency.y.config_path, include.x.inputs.p) or from get_parent_terragrunt_dir(), and 6 extra edges where find_in_parent_folders finds the file at two ancestors and Terragrunt takes the nearer (one file cannot see which ancestors have it); an included file's relative paths are left unresolved (they are read from the including unit)", trigger: "a tier-A dump of a Terragrunt file's blocks with lines, or a stack render (terragrunt stack generate offline) and a fixtures scope the owner accepts for the attestation oracle; fusion.ResolveDocXrefs choosing the nearest of several repo-path candidates (tl); read_terragrunt_config edges, which terragrunt find --reading reports per unit, not per entity"}

crossplane composition render needs the functions' runtimes), so L2 and L3 cap at good under the tier-C walk; a composed resource in a function's input and a provider's kinds need schemas from a registry (2 composed resources unevidenced); a composite resource or claim of a user-defined API group stays Kubernetes, so the claim-to-XRD edge needs a shared Kubernetes-dialect join from a group and kind to the CRD or XRD declaring it, coordinated with wave pc's constraint-to-ConstraintTemplate join; the engine cannot split an apiVersion into its group, so a composite type is matched by kind alone (28 ambiguous kinds in crossplane/crossplane's e2e manifests draw no edge); a file pairing an XRD with its example composite resource stays Kubernetes whole (CRMixed counts 2); a managed resource's patches (fromFieldPath) are not read", trigger: "an offline evaluator of composition references (crossplane composition render with the functions' runtimes, asked first) and the function and provider schemas fetched from a registry (asked first); a shared kubernetes-dialect `instance_of` join (group and kind to CRD or XRD) landing with wave pc's constraint join; a group-aware value in the doc engine"}

The 39 out-of-scope files name a compose service nginx cannot resolve here, a certificate, a module or an NGINX Plus directive; xref_recall .9231: an include glob (conf.d/*.conf) names no file, as fusion.ResolveDocXrefs matches a glob against directories; 12 of h5bp's 38 files open with more than a KiB of comments and the .conf sniff leaves them ambiguous (disclosed); an extensionless proxy/conf and envsubst *.conf.template files are claimed by no name", trigger: "an ablation attestation of include edges and upstream references in nginx_oracle.py (tier A); docxref.go matching a glob against files (tl); the sniff skipping leading comment lines; a templates/*.conf.template convention claim"}

testdata copied as td because Discover prunes it), 2026-10-02: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (821 definitions), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (10 written foreign-key pairs, tier C), xref_precision and xref_recall 1 (12 route pairs, a service host or upstream target to the compose service declaring it, all found by fathom), so L1 excellent, L2 good and L3 excellent {id: LB-Q-kong-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "tier C caps L2 at good: decK renders a route under its service and a plugin under its scope, so the written reference (a route's `service`, a plugin's `service` or `route`) is read from the text and only cross-checked by the tool, and a route written under its service is containment the doc engine, which reads downward from an entity, cannot draw as a reference (O347); only 10 such pairs are written across the 821 definitions. supabase/supabase's kong.yml is the DB-less loader's (acls, credentials and comments): decK refuses it, so its 14 route pairs are tier C and its definitions unattested, and it is not scored (lang-oracle would count the dialect's definitions against an empty oracle); 1,011 of the 1,358 files decK accepts in Kong/deck (224 of 437 in go-database-reconciler) are not named kong* (O348)", trigger: "a Kong-native reading of foreign keys that says which were written (Kong's own DB-less loader run on the file), or a parent-binding selector in the doc engine so a nested route's service is a reference (O347); an oracle that attests a DB-less file (decK or Kong's loader accepting acls and credentials), which would score Supabase's 14 pairs at tier C"}

- [-] Traefik static and file-provider configuration beyond L2: measured against traefik 3.7.13 (its file provider, `GET /api/rawdata` and `/api/entrypoints` on a scratch copy; tier A, calibration/oracles/traefik/README.md) on 12 repositories with Traefik files named where the manifest claims them (19 files), 2026-10-02: sym_recall 1, sym_precision .9934, ref_precision 1 and ref_recall .9643 (81 of 84 pairs), so L1 excellent and L2 excellent; L3 is unmet: xref_precision .7241 and xref_recall 1 (21 route pairs, a loadBalancer server to the compose service declaring it, all found, 29 drawn) {id: LB-Q-traefik-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "xref_precision .7241 is under the .90 bar: fathom draws 8 pairs beyond the truth for wmde/wikibase-suite, joining its config/traefik-dynamic.yml routes to the five development/tests overlay stacks beside the root compose file, the route resolver's repository fallback (O435); the xref family is also tier C (Bin-hy/Vistack's route joins a Kubernetes Service read by yaml.v3), which would cap L3 at good. ref_recall .9643 clears the .95 bar only through the larger denominator: its 3 missed pairs are still written `service: x@file` and `- y@file`, the file provider's suffix, and the doc engine's value expression has no string operation to strip it (O349). Static-to-dynamic file edges are unscored: every static file in a subject names its dynamic file by a container path (/etc/traefik/dynamic.yml, /configuration), which resolves through the compose volume mount, not as a repository path. The first eight repositories joined 4 route pairs, under the 10-pair floor; ecamp/ecamp3, picodotdev/blog-ejemplos, rendiffdev/rendiff-dev and wmde/wikibase-suite were added. traefik/traefik's own docs and integration fixtures are named for what they test and are not claimed by a name", trigger: "the resolver walking ancestor directories before the repository (O435, wave ic); a doc-engine value operation that strips a provider suffix, or a dialect-side alias (O349); a compose-volume join from a container path to the mounted file"}
- [x] `indexbench lang-routes` restricts only the measured dialect's files to `-only`, never the compose and Kubernetes files a route target joins to: a route file named by -only still resolves against its compose files, and fathom's pairs and unresolved references count for the files in scope; picosh/pico's Caddyfiles (named by -only) join 20 pairs, all found {id: LB-F168, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/caddyfile.json, pattern: "\"route_truth_found\": 20"}
- [x] The proxy oracles' route rule: a compose project is a directory, so a route joins the declarations of its nearest project that declares the host (its own directory, then each ancestor), one pair per compose file of that directory that declares the service, compose files read by docker compose config; the haproxy reading takes 19 route pairs from five subjects at tier A and the envoy, kong, traefik, caddyfile and nginx oracles share the rule (calibration/oracles/*/README.md) {id: LB-F169, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/haproxy.json, pattern: "a route joins the declarations of its nearest compose project"}
- [x] The nginx oracle is tier B: crossplane 0.5.8 relabelled from C, reasoned in calibration/oracles/nginx/README.md (NGINX Inc's parser tool on the envoy README ladder, design 1.5 D2's independent parser dump), earned by nginx -T 1.31.6 loading each file in scope and crossplane parsing the same directives from its dump, disagreements and unloadable files counted out; L1 reads excellent {id: LB-F170, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/nginx.json, pattern: "crossplane 0.5.8 is tier B .LB-F170."}
- [x] C# names a generic method's callee without its type arguments: `Make<int>(2)` is `Make`, `Registry.Pair<System.Exception, string>(5)` is `Registry.Pair` (never the `string>` tail a dotted type argument left), member, static and conditional-access forms included; call_precision .7776 to .794 against scip-dotnet 0.2.14 on Polly + Newtonsoft.Json; product-reached since `.cs` is dispatched to the dialect (LB-F232, wave lh; O462 closed) {id: LB-F189, symbol: tsCalleeName, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestCSharpDialectGenericCallees, needs: "LB-F4,LB-F65"}
- [x] C# `nameof(x)` is an operator, not a call: the dialect no longer lists it (368 of the former fathom-only pairs); product-reached as LB-F189 is (LB-F232) {id: LB-F190, symbol: tsCalleeName, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestCSharpDialectNameofIsNotACall, needs: "LB-F4"}
- [x] C# calls are qualified under the manifest's `calls.qualify` as PHP's are (LB-F65): a member or static call is its receiver and the name (`reg.Add`, `Registry.Create`, `this.Plain`, `base.ToString`, `reg.Touch` for `reg?.Touch<int>()`), a delegate invocation its identifier, `new X(...)` no call (scip-dotnet writes it as a `.ctor` reference, O463); product-reached as LB-F189 is (LB-F232) {id: LB-F191, symbol: qualifiedCallee, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestCSharpDialectQualifiedCalls, needs: "LB-F65"}
- [x] PHP's call spelling is pinned and its L2 gap is counted: the dialect writes `self::make`, `parent::run`, `Helper::assist`, `this.client.send`, `other.items` (nullsafe), a plain function and no call for `new X`, and each of the 1,869 pairs scip-php lacks was read at its written site (1,726 a method on a variable receiver; all oracle-blind, O467) {id: LB-F192, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/php.json, pattern: "L2 re-measure 2026-10-02 .wave ld"}
- [x] C# L2 re-measured with the fixed call query and the 7,498 fathom-only pairs classified: call_precision .794, call_recall .9448, 5,272 generic-method calls with explicit type arguments, 754 in #if regions, 517 delegate invocations, 176 delegate-typed members, 185 in invalid-global-code documents, 594 unclassified, each counted once (O466) {id: LB-F193, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/csharp.json, pattern: "L2 re-measure 2026-10-02 .wave ld"}

- [-] Vim script refused (design §14.2): the dialect is built and measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on junegunn/vim-plug 0.14.0 and tpope/vim-fugitive v3.7 (7 files) at L1 excellent (sym_recall .9947, sym_precision 1, kind_agreement 1, range_agreement 1, signature_coverage 1, 0 faults), but not dispatched: .vim and the vimrc basenames stay unextracted, .vim stays disclosed as unsupported and no level is claimed {id: LB-Q-vimscript-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real Vim script under the 5% line: 3 of the 7 calibration files (42.9%: vim-plug's plug.vim, vim-fugitive's autoload/fugitive.vim and plugin/fugitive.vim) parse with an error under tree-sitter-grammars/tree-sitter-vim v0.8.1, and upstream master (two commits past the tag) reads the same, on an embedded Python heredoc, a bare - ex command and a unary-plus string; the 3 definitions the dialect misses (s:update_python, the commands G and Git) sit inside those regions; Re-plan when a tree-sitter-vim commit or release parses both subjects with at most 5% of files in error: re-measure, then dispatch .vim and the vimrc basenames (an ExtractorEpoch bump); vim-fugitive's licence (the terms of Vim itself, not OSI-approved) needs a decision first"}

- [-] Prisma beyond L3: measured against the Prisma schema engine (prisma-schema-wasm 6.19.0, getDMMF, tier A, calibration/oracles/prisma-dmmf) on calcom/cal.com v5.9.9 (2 files), hiroppy/web-app-template main (3 files) and dubinc/dub main (37 files) at fathom 999d94bc: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (3,770 definitions), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (1,146 pairs), xref_recall and xref_precision 1 (303 of 303, the global-name strategy scoped to a schema folder, LB-F580), 332 of 332 stored cross-file pairs at ingest; holdouts mfts/papermark L3 excellent (162 of 162) and prisma/prisma-examples (no cross-file truth, no cross-file edge drawn) {id: LB-Q-prisma-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L3 is met at excellent; what is left is the pinned grammar: a /** ... */ comment inside a model, view or type block is an error to victorhqc/tree-sitter-prisma, which loses the members after it (the papermark holdout: 4 of 1,388 definitions, 2 of 466 pairs, O1439); no tuning subject has one", trigger: "a grammar pin that parses a block comment inside a block, or a pre-parse step that blanks one"}

- [-] Tcl refused (design §14.2): the dialect is built and measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on tcltk/tcllib tcllib-2-0 and tcltk/tk core-9-0-4 (827 files) but reads no level (sym_recall .8047, sym_precision .7453, kind_agreement 1, range_agreement .9703, 1 fault), and is not dispatched: .tcl, .tk and .tm stay unextracted, .tcl stays disclosed as unsupported and no level is claimed {id: LB-Q-tcl-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real Tcl under the 5% line: 516 of the 827 calibration files (62.4%: tcllib 438 of 704, Tk 78 of 123) parse with an error under tree-sitter-grammars/tree-sitter-tcl 850a72ab, upstream main's head and the best pin measured (two older commits read 62.7% to 69.1%), because the grammar reads every braced word as a script and fails on an array element such as $a($i), a !flag word and a stray semicolon; L1 is also unmet on the metrics, mostly because ctags names a namespace by the whole path it is written with (::struct::list) and the harness compares the oracle's names unreduced, so 759 namespaces and classes never pair (a scratch run reducing them reads sym_recall .9292, sym_precision .8607); Re-plan when a tree-sitter-tcl commit parses both subjects with at most 5% of files in error, then re-measure (with the harness comparing the oracle's names by last segment as it does the dialect's) and dispatch .tcl, .tk and .tm (an ExtractorEpoch bump)"}

- [-] Conftest taken up 2026-10-02 (wave pc) once LB-Q-rego proved [x]: its policies, `.rego` under `policy/` or any directory a configuration names, are read by the Rego dialect (LB-F131) at Rego's level; no conftest dialect is built and no reading taken {id: LB-Q-conftest-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "the L2 target has no truth to measure: a conftest.toml holds no reference within the file (its keys, policy, namespace, data, combine, parser, output, name directories and Rego namespaces in other files), so ref_recall and ref_precision have no pair on any subject, and the installed conftest (a dev build on OPA 1.15.2) has no command that reports its configuration (parse prints an input file as data, verify runs the policies' unit tests, doc renders their METADATA, test evaluates inputs); the keys are already symbols through the generic TOML extractor, so a doc dialect for them adds an L1 of two keys on the pinned subject (open-policy-agent/conftest holds one conftest.toml: policy and namespace); the queue's tags+doc engine assumed a Rego grammar, moot now that Rego is native", trigger: "the owner retargets the item: L1 for the configuration's keys on subjects with several conftest.toml files, or L3 for its references (a policy directory and a namespace to the Rego files declaring the package, which needs a doc-xref resolution that reads Rego packages: .rego is not a doc candidate and no doc strategy names a package)"}

- [-] Cedar refused (design §14.2), 2026-10-02 (wave pc): the subjects (calibration/corpus.d/cedar.yaml: cedar-policy/cedar-examples c251f7d1, 34 files, and cedar-policy/cedar-integration-tests v4.13.0, 25 files; Apache-2.0) and the tier-A oracle (calibration/oracles/cedar, cedar-policy-cli 4.13.0, which accepts all 59 files: 168 policies and templates, 84 entity types, 148 actions, 9 common types, 3 namespaces; 421 schema reference pairs; 664 scope references) are committed; no dialect is built or dispatched and .cedar and .cedarschema stay disclosed as unsupported {id: LB-Q-cedar-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no parser in scope reads Cedar under the 5% line: chrnorm/tree-sitter-cedar 5092204d (MIT by its LICENCE, indexbench lang-pin -tree; package.json declares ISC) leaves an ERROR node in 5 of the 42 policy files the CLI accepts (11.9%: a namespaced action entity, `PhotoApp::Action::\"viewPhoto\"`, in three; an index by a string key, `x[\"Role-A\"]`; an annotation whose string spans lines) and has no grammar for the 17 schema files, so the schema half (namespaces, entity types, actions, common types: L1's required kinds) has no definitions at all; cedar-go v1.8.0 (cda92d0d, Apache-2.0, +867,680 bytes on cmd/fathom) rejects 6 of the 42 (14.3%: every file holding a template, which NewPolicySetFromBytes does not read) and its schema AST (x/exp/schema/ast) carries no source position, so no schema definition has a line to be matched or ranged by; measured with the tree-sitter CLI 0.27.0 on the pinned grammar (not the lock recipe's wasm build) and a scratch Go program on cedar-go; a reader of Cedar's own (Rego's route, ADR-048's wave-pc amendment) was outside this item's scope; L3 has no import statement to measure (import_resolution and file_edge need one), so Cedar's cross-file reach is the governed scope, a policy_ref from a policy to the schema file declaring the type or action, which the oracle writes as cross_references", trigger: "the owner admits a native Cedar reader under ADR-048's wave-pc amendment (the second language to take it, its revisit trigger), or a tree-sitter-cedar commit with a schema grammar and at most 5% of CLI-accepted files in error, or a cedar-go release that reads templates and gives schema declarations positions; then the dialect is built (policies by @id, else file:index; namespaces, entity types, actions, common types; the governed scope as policy_ref, strategy service-name), measured against the committed oracle, and dispatched with an ExtractorEpoch bump; the queue kind (language, L3 by import edges Cedar does not have) is the owner's call"}

- [-] Azure Policy beyond L2 good: measured against an independent JSON walk of the definition schema's structure (tier C, its own line-tracking JSON reader) on Azure/azure-policy 7b0fa25a (5,658 policy documents: 5,387 definitions, 271 initiatives), 2026-10-02: sym_recall 1, sym_precision .9999 (55,711 of 55,714 definitions: 3 dialect-only parameters, O394), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (21,137 `[parameters('x')]` pairs), range_agreement 1, so L1 good and L2 good; L3 (the resource-type policy_ref emission) is not measured {id: LB-Q-azure-policy-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "tier C caps every metric at good (§1.3): no Azure tool evaluates a definition offline (O391), and the JSON schema checks shape, not the entities; the resource-type policy_ref (field: type with equals, in or like) is emitted and pinned by goldens, but its targets are ARM and Bicep resources that wave iac declares, so until then the resolver reports them undeclared, and no scorer reads them here; re-examined in wave ox: Microsoft's Enterprise Policy as Code (EnterprisePolicyAsCode 11.5.8, Azure/enterprise-azure-policy-as-code, MIT) reads a definition offline only as ConvertFrom-Json and property reads (Build-PolicyPlan, Convert-PolicyToDetails: the name, the parameters, the effect), the same reading as the walk, with no source lines and no list of parameters('x') uses, and its plan builder (Build-DeploymentPlans) reads the deployed policy resources through an Azure connection; the Bicep CLI's policyDefinitions type check accepts or refuses a document and names no entity; so the walk stays tier C (never relabelled) and the ceiling stands", trigger: "an offline Azure Policy evaluator that lists a definition's entities with source lines (tier A), or the ARM/Bicep dialects merged so a resource type resolves to a declared resource"}
- [x] Cloud Custodian policies (policies YAML) — tool format at L2 (doc engine; yaml.v3, n/a; oracle: custodian validate (tier A, offline)) {id: LB-Q-cloud-custodian, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/cloud-custodian.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[234]\"", needs: "LB-F8,LB-F9,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F21,LB-F22"}
- [-] Cloud Custodian beyond L2 (L1 and L2 excellent): measured against c7n 0.9.53 (tier A: its own loader, schema validator and filter and action registries; lines from a PyYAML composition, tier C) on cloud-custodian/cloud-custodian bf126f38 (13 of its 19 policy files: -only drops the 4 fixtures written to fail and 2 files discovery does not read), 2026-10-02: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (27 policies), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (28 filter and action type pairs), range_agreement 1, so L1 excellent and L2 excellent; L3 (the resource-type policy_ref emission) is not measured {id: LB-Q-cloud-custodian-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "the queue's filters and actions as entities are carried as the policy's references instead: an entity name is a value on its own node and a symbol key is service::package.Name, so a file's second value filter would collide (O392); the subject's policy YAML is 6 KB, so the throughput gate reads 0.68 to 2.01 MB/s run to run (O393) and the committed reading is a run that met it; policy_ref targets come from the committed tier-A registry map (LB-F140), and the resolver is the policy-reference item's", trigger: "an ancestor value in an entity name (the engine), or a larger YAML subject of Custodian policies"}
